Execution follows fn-77 in the delivery order and\\nre-anchors its final Producer/Case interfaces before edits; this source coordination is not a new\\nsemantic dependency on parameterized field Properties.\\n\\n### Narrow prerequisite amendments\\n\\nInspection identified three concrete gaps in the integration required below. This spec includes\\ntheir smallest fixes in the existing owners, followed by canary integration:\\n\\n- Add `PreparedCase.ValidateDriver(ctx, driver) error` to the public Testpilot facade. Extract and\\n  reuse the existing preflight identity/binding and `Driver.Validate` checks without creating a\\n  Monitor, Session, Run, or effect. `Run` repeats current preflight validation; an earlier successful\\n  check grants no lasting execution authority. Canary validates every selected prepared Case and\\n  Driver before scheduling, without duplicating private execution rules.\\n- Ensure the checked Nexus3 Producer supplies a finite target `workflow_execution_timeout` of\\n  60 seconds. Current request construction omits a server lifetime. Make this amendment in the\\n  Producer, regenerate and explicitly repin both consumers, and qualify lowering/provenance/trust.\\n  Preserve the success Property and correlated Contract; canary never patches requests. If fn-77\\n  already delivers the required backstop, reuse and qualify it instead of adding another timeout.\\n- Make the CHASM scheduler honor its existing namespace-scoped\\n  `scheduler.tweakables.CanceledTerminatedCountAsFailures` setting when applying `PauseOnFailure`.\\n  Its pause predicate currently ignores that field. Preserve the default `false` behavior and\\n  all other statuses. The dedicated local canary namespace requires CHASM scheduling with this\\n  field explicitly `true`; live tests prove cancellation and termination pause future ticks.\\n\\nThese amendments do not reopen unrelated completed work, add an operation-cancellation capability,\\nor introduce another scheduler/recovery service. They belong to their existing facade, Producer,\\nand scheduler owners, with focused tests before integration.\\n\\n## API Contracts\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\nA check selection names a known catalog entry, pinned artifact digest and environment binding. Each entry supplies one bounded Case and uses the same generic execution path. Empty selection starts no schedules; unknown names, duplicate selections, digest mismatch, unsupported Cases and incompatible environment bindings reject before activating schedules or dispatching a check. No auto-discovery, random selection, exploration or arbitrary executable selection.\\n\\nProvide the smallest operator interface that can list supported checks, apply an explicit selection, run the worker, pause/resume an owned schedule and inspect check status. Schedule reconciliation has stable IDs scoped to this canary installation and check identity. Reapplying the same selection does not create duplicates; a conflicting existing schedule is reported rather than silently taken over. List/status/worker startup do not create schedules. Removing a check from an explicitly applied selection pauses its owned schedule; it does not delete historical measurements or unrelated schedules.\\n\\nUse one Temporal Schedule per selected check with a 60-second interval and overlap policy Skip. Each tick starts a fresh Check Workflow; do not implement cadence as sleep-after-completion in a permanent workflow. A slow check causes skipped ticks rather than queued overlapping executions. Use a bounded catch-up window so recovery does not replay a backlog of missed minutes. The exact supported SDK setting and minimum catch-up behavior must be verified against the pinned SDK during implementation and documented.\\n\\nSet catch-up explicitly to 10 seconds and verify the effective Schedule policy with Describe.\\nThe pinned SDK comments and server defaults differ; no default is assumed. Public API documentation\\nalso specifies a 10-second minimum and a one-year default, so zero is unsuitable for this prototype.\\nSee [SchedulePolicies](https://pkg.go.dev/go.temporal.io/api/schedule/v1#SchedulePolicies).\\n\\nThe Check Workflow invokes one bounded Activity that prepares/obtains the pinned Case and calls PreparedCase.Run through the shared Driver. It does not interpret the Program itself. Disable Workflow execution retries and set the check Activity's maximum attempts to one; normal Workflow Task replay remains supported. One scheduled tick identifies one measurement, not a retry-until-green loop. Do not claim exactly-once external effects merely from these retry settings.\\n\\nA completed measurement retains check identity, orchestration Workflow/run identity, Case digest and producer provenance, Profile/catalog identity, Testpilot Run identity, separate execution disposition, Verdict and cleanup status, plus a reference to the retained Run. Return only a bounded summary in Workflow history and keep full Run data in a configured bounded local artifact sink for the prototype. Define finite byte/count retention and observable publication failures; use atomic publication for files and preserve the owning module's path-safety conventions. No credentials or opaque completion capabilities enter reporting. A reporting failure cannot trigger another execution.\\n\\nModel violation and inconclusive results are measurements, not retryable Activity failures. Operational inability to obtain an authoritative result is recorded distinctly as uncertain/incomplete at the canary layer. Never synthesize a satisfied Verdict, successful cleanup or a closed Testpilot Run after process loss.\\n\\n### Selection, ownership, and reporting\\n\\nThe supported catalog initially contains exactly `nexus3-success`. Operator configuration supplies\\nan installation identity, pinned artifact digest, explicit environment bindings, and caller-owned\\nconnection/provisioning policy. A production catalog may define its own public ProfileSpec policy;\\nit cannot import the functional test fixture helper or infer new product assertions from a Case.\\n\\nThe command surface is `list`, `apply`, `worker`, `pause`, `resume`, and `status`. `apply` receives\\nthe complete explicit selection; empty selection pauses previously owned checks. Validate every\\nselection and inspect every existing schedule conflict before mutation. Stage new schedules paused\\nand enable only after the whole selection has validated and staging succeeds. Reapply preserves\\noperator/failure pauses; only explicit `resume` can clear them. Revalidate ownership and expected\\nconfiguration inside updates. Network failure can leave partial state: report exact acknowledged\\nmutations and uncertainty, never claim reconciliation is a cross-schedule transaction.\\n\\nSchedule ownership combines stable installation/check IDs with a versioned configuration identity.\\nArtifact or binding changes require explicit update input. Neither apply nor resume triggers an\\nimmediate run or backfill. Removing a selection preserves retained measurements and unrelated\\nschedules. Pause scheduling before operator cancellation or shutdown and report an unacknowledged\\npause as uncertain. Server pause-on-failure remains necessary when no worker is available to act.\\n\\nUse the existing immutable artifact publication owner under `tools/common/artifactio`. Retain the\\nactual Run and measurement metadata together under an immutable reference. Default limits are\\n16 KiB for Workflow summaries, 8 MiB per measurement, 128 retained measurements, and 256 MiB total.\\nBound decoding and encoding; reserve count/byte capacity before dispatch and reject exhaustion\\nwithout executing again or overwriting prior measurements. Coordinate capacity reservations across\\nconcurrent writers to the same owned sink, or reject a second sink owner explicitly; a Go mutex\\nalone does not protect separate processes. No new distributed lease service is needed.\\n\\nReserve a durable local measurement identity before invoking Run. Duplicate or unresolved claimed\\nidentities cannot dispatch again; a retained identical result can be read without re-execution.\\nCrash leftovers remain accounted for until explicit reconciliation. Reclaiming storage does not\\nauthorize another attempt, and these local rules do not promise exactly-once external effects.\\n\\nPublication failure is an operational measurement/reporting failure and causes failed orchestration\\nto pause scheduling. Preserve any authoritative Run already produced and distinguish publication\\nambiguity from absence. A late durable publication cannot change a timed-out Workflow's outcome,\\noverwrite another measurement, or cause redispatch. Full Run data, credentials, and opaque effect\\ncapabilities never enter Workflow history; producer provenance there is bounded and digest-bound.\\n\\n### Time and resource budgets\\n\\nThe current Case allows 30 seconds of execution and three distinct 5-second termination, cleanup,\\nand Close phases. Budget 45 seconds before reporting, not merely execution plus one cleanup phase.\\nDefault Activity StartToClose is 60 seconds, ScheduleToClose 75 seconds, and Check Workflow execution\\nand run timeouts 90 seconds. Publication has a 10-second bound. Heartbeat every 5 seconds with a\\n20-second HeartbeatTimeout; wait for Activity cancellation so live-worker cleanup can finish.\\nRecompute the budget from fn-77's delivered Case and reject incompatible larger limits rather than\\nsilently tightening execution semantics. Target server lifetime is independently bounded as above.\\n\\nUse worker concurrency one for the initial one-check catalog, bounded pollers, and no local dispatch\\nqueue. Drain within 60 seconds and close worker-owned clients/Driver resources with an explicit\\nfinal bound. Status reports actual scheduled/started/completed timestamps and treats a result older\\nthan three minutes as stale; a fresh violated result remains violated. A stopped worker, unreachable\\ncluster, missing result, stale result, and failed publication remain distinct from Verdict.\\n\\nThe local deployment uses dedicated orchestration resources and explicitly configured CHASM\\ncreation, 100% creation rollout, routing, required sentinel setup, and the cancellation/termination\\npause setting. Use the existing server configuration and test-cluster mechanisms; do not change\\nglobal defaults, migrate unrelated schedules, or deploy infrastructure as part of writing this plan.\\nVerify canceled and terminated runs automatically pause and allow no subsequent tick before explicit\\nresume. A manual status warning alone does not satisfy R8.\\n\\n## Edge Cases & Constraints\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n- Replay: workflow replay must not invoke Testpilot or duplicate external work. Activity results, including violated/inconclusive Verdicts, are replayed as data.\\n- Process loss/timeouts: the Activity may have caused effects even if its response is lost. Mark the measurement uncertain, propagate orchestration failure and configure the schedule to pause on such failure/timeouts. No automatic redispatch or automatic resume. The operator inspects and reconciles exact owned resources before resuming. This prototype needs no general recovery service.\\n- Cancellation/cleanup: cancellation reaches Testpilot's bounded cleanup path when the worker is alive. Give the Activity enough time for execution plus cleanup and the Workflow enough time for the Activity terminal result. Target workflows/resources need finite server-enforced lifetimes as a backstop when the Activity worker disappears. A timeout is not evidence that all effects stopped; schedule Skip alone cannot establish resource isolation.\\n- Shared process: reuse immutable prepared Cases and driver registrations, but each measurement has distinct Run identity, Slots, captures, capabilities and resource ownership. A late result cannot mutate another measurement. Cap worker concurrency explicitly for the small configured selection; a 10x tick/load increase cannot create an unbounded dispatch queue or retained-output growth.\\n- Dependency failure: scheduling and target execution may use the same local cluster for the demonstration. A cluster outage can prevent measurement creation. Status inspection must expose last scheduled/started/completed observations and freshness separately from Verdict. Unreachable or stale status is never healthy. This is inspection by an external operator/client, not a claim that an unavailable cluster can report its own outage.\\n- Resource portability: reuse the same exact Case bytes across two distinct namespace/task-queue bindings. Binding changes alter the prepared/Driver binding identity, but not the source artifact, checked success requirement, Contract, provenance, or correlation interpretation. Unsupported bindings fail explicitly.\\n- Shutdown: stop new owned scheduling when requested and drain/close worker-owned resources within configured bounds. Do not clean unrelated workflows or queues. Preserve independent cleanup and Verdict outcomes.\\n- Schema evolution: consume the Testpilot protocol and fn-68 Producer as evolved by the binding and typed temporal authoring prerequisites. This spec owns no protocol extraction, expression-language redesign, temporal semantics, evidence projection, or binding wire change.\\n\\n## Acceptance Criteria\\n<!-- scope: both -->\\n\\n- **R1:** `tools/canary` exposes only explicitly selected supported checks, initially Nexus3 success. Unknown/duplicate names, empty selection, stale or mismatched artifacts and unauthorized/incompatible bindings are covered by tests; invalid selections create no active schedule or target Run.\\n- **R2:** Canary and the existing functional test consume the fn-68 Producer's checked success behavior through the same Testpilot facade. Source IDs/fingerprints and exact Known Gaps remain bound to checked inputs. No duplicated monitor, Nexus assertion, scenario interpreter, or runtime Lean invocation exists in canary.\\n- **R3:** One shared Temporal Driver serves both consumers from outside `tests/`, preserving server/worker/delivery ownership. Dependency checks show canary and shared Driver production code import neither `tests/`, Umpire generator tools nor private Testpilot execution/verification packages. Canary integration preserves the prerequisite Driver guarantees; its extraction and migration tests remain owned by `fn-72-extract-the-reusable-temporal-testpilot`.\\n- **R4:** The same checked selection executes against two explicit namespace/task-queue configurations using one binding mechanism and isolated run-owned resources. Neither consumer rewrites prepared requests or independently hard-codes corresponding worker/request bindings; mismatch tests fail before check dispatch.\\n- **R5:** Applying an explicit selection creates one stable, 60-second, Skip-overlap Schedule per check, starting a fresh Workflow for each measurement. Reapply is idempotent, conflicts reject, removal pauses only owned schedules, and pause/resume work. A check exceeding the interval does not overlap or accumulate buffered starts; catch-up is bounded and tested.\\n- **R6:** Workflow orchestration contains only deterministic SDK operations and calls a bounded Activity for Testpilot execution. Workflow execution retries and check Activity retries are disabled. Replay tests show no duplicate external dispatch; a violated or inconclusive measurement is retained without an automatic rerun.\\n- **R7:** Repeated completed measurements retain distinct Run/resource identities and unchanged semantics, with separate disposition, Verdict, cleanup and bounded artifact references. Cross-run isolation, late completions, reporting failure and retention exhaustion have focused tests; none overwrites a prior measurement or causes redispatch.\\n- **R8:** Activity worker loss, cancellation and timeouts cannot fabricate Run closure or success. Uncertain orchestration failures pause the schedule until explicit resume; bounded cleanup and finite target resource lifetimes are demonstrated. Status inspection distinguishes missing/stale measurements and unavailable orchestration from actual violated/inconclusive Verdicts.\\n- **R9:** A live local demonstration runs the existing Nexus3 functional test and at least two successive scheduled canary measurements through the shared Driver. Successful canary Runs have completed disposition, satisfied Verdict, successful cleanup and the Producer-defined correlated history support. Assert event-driven results rather than fixed test sleeps; use a generous bounded deadline for the real minute cadence.\\n- **R10:** Documentation under `tools/canary` gives reproducible build/artifact provisioning, explicit selection, worker, schedule apply/pause/resume/status and cleanup commands; names required environment settings, finite limits, retry/overlap behavior, result storage and the same-cluster freshness limitation. It clearly identifies the non-production proof-of-concept scope and fn-68 dependency.\\n\\n## Boundaries\\n<!-- scope: business -->\\n\\nNo production deployment, customer traffic, release qualification, protected GitHub workflow, general lease/fencing/recovery platform, dashboard, alerting service, remote result store, or fn-29 receipt machinery. Three failures fire at base\\ncommits in about one or two suite runs in ten. Workers re-run the gate until it passes and record\\nthe failure as a \\\"known flake\\\". A gate that is red for no reason trains people to re-run it, and a\\nre-run can also hide a real regression. The failures, as the fn-87 receipts recorded them\\n(2026-09-12 and 2026-09-13):\\n\\n1. **Namespace-delete timeout.** `TestTestpilotUmpireRunRunsACheckedInCaseAgainstAnyEndpoint`\\n   failed with a namespace-delete `DeadlineExceeded` (fn-87.1, twice in three gate runs).\\n2. **Evidence-ordering mismatch.** `TestTestpilotTypedNexusOperationsCase` failed with an\\n   evidence-ordering mismatch described as \\\"history interleaving\\\". It failed in 2 of 4 gate runs\\n   (fn-87.6) and passed 5/5 when run alone. It also failed alongside (1) in fn-87.1.\\n3. **Async-Nexus Run not SATISFIED.** An async-Nexus Run ended INCONCLUSIVE, or with Run\\n   disposition INCOMPLETE, instead of SATISFIED. This was \\\"known flake (c)\\\" in fn-87.7, .8, .9,\\n   .13, .15 and .16. It hit `TestTestpilotAsyncNexusCase`,\\n   `TestTestpilotAsyncNexusCaseRunsFromItsFixtureNameAlone`, the plain peer Run in\\n   `TestTestpilotWorkerOutageCaseLeavesAnotherQueueAlone`, and the umpire-run test. Measured\\n   rates: `-count=5` over two tests failed 1 of 10 at base and 2 of 10 at HEAD (fn-87.16).\\n   Repeating in-process with `-count=4` raised the rate (fn-87.7).\\n\\nSince then fn-86 renamed or retired these identities. The typed Nexus test became\\n`TestTestpilotNexusPairCase` (the `nexusPairTests-bothComplete` fixture). The async Case became\\n`TestTestpilotNexusCallerAsyncCompletion`, which runs under the hsm and chasm switch values with\\nfour concurrent Runs each; the umpire-run test drives the same `nexusCallerTests-asyncCompletion`\\nfixture; `TestTestpilotAsyncNexusCaseRunsFromItsFixtureNameAlone` became\\n`TestTestpilotNexusCallerCaseRunsFromItsFixtureNameAlone`. No receipt after 2026-09-13 records\\nthese failures. `UMPIRE4_ORDER.md` no longer lists them as carried-forward failures; its delivery\\nqueue carries this spec in their place. This spec first re-measures the failures on today's\\nidentities, then resolves each one at its cause.\\n\\n## Architecture & Data Models\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n- **Reproduction harness.** A developer command runs a named `^TestTestpilot...` selection N times\\n  and emits a per-iteration record plus a summary grouped by failure signature. A **failure\\n  signature** has these fields: test identity, failing assertion text, Run disposition, Verdict\\n  status, the status and terminal state of each rule that did not satisfy, the Run diagnostics\\n  (kind and code), and the stderr leak lines. Two modes: one process per iteration (`-count=1`),\\n  and one process with `-count=N`. The test binary is built once before the first iteration. The\\n  tests still read things from the working tree at run time: Case fixtures, the Lean helper\\n  binaries, and the umpire commands some tests build with `go build`. So before every iteration\\n  the harness fingerprints those inputs and stops the loop, keeping the completed iterations, when\\n  the fingerprint differs from the one it started with. A loop therefore measures one commit or\\n  says it could not. The harness reads the\\n  `go test -json` event stream, not the text output. The harness only observes and never changes\\n  the suite. It lives outside the gate, behind its own make target that sets up the same\\n  prerequisites the gate does (the Lean helper binaries and the physical temporary directory).\\n- **Signature line.** The harness and the live tests meet at one line format (see API Contracts).\\n  A failing live assertion prints the signature line; the harness reads it from the test's output\\n  events. A failing test that prints no signature line still gets a signature, built from the test\\n  identity and its first failing assertion line with every other field empty. This split lets the\\n  harness and the instrumentation be built at the same time.\\n- **Run capture.** A signature says why a Run failed but carries no timing. To size a bound\\n  (path 3a) from passing and failing Runs alike, the affected tests write every closed Run to a\\n  directory when the harness names one in the environment, the same way the control and canary\\n  tests already record Runs on request. The harness keeps the captured Run paths in its record.\\n- **Signature-bearing failures.** Every live assertion on a Run disposition or Verdict in the\\n  affected tests prints the Run disposition, the Run diagnostics and each rule's status. A single\\n  failing run is then diagnosable without a rerun. Some pair and caller assertions already print\\n  diagnostics; this spec makes that uniform for the affected identities.\\n- **Current code paths per failure.**\\n  - (1) The umpire-run `--create` binding provisions a namespace and deletes it on exit. The\\n    operator `DeleteNamespace` handler starts the system delete-namespace workflow and waits for\\n    its result. The functional cluster these tests use runs no system worker (`testcore` turns it\\n    off by default, and `WithWorkerService` is required to turn it on). The delete therefore always\\n    blocks until the binding's 30 s per-release teardown budget ends; every passing run of this test\\n    takes about 35 s. The test says it does not assert this deletion, so the failure comes from a\\n    side effect of the stuck delete, not from the delete result. The failing assertion was never\\n    recorded. The candidates are: the test logger failing on an error-level log from the stuck\\n    delete (the server-side versus client-side deadline race); the test's 2-minute process bound\\n    killing the CLI during teardown, since the Run's own `--timeout` is also 2 minutes and teardown\\n    adds 30 s on top; or an assertion on the CLI output. Releases run in reverse creation order, so\\n    the endpoint is deleted before the namespace. That order matters: the delete-namespace workflow\\n    refuses a namespace that a Nexus endpoint still targets.\\n  - (2) Two operations are scheduled in one workflow task. Their completions reach history in an\\n    order set by timing. The retired test asserted the lifted `CorrelatedEvidence` kinds in one\\n    fixed global order. The pair Model's Scenario is an exact sequence over two instances. The\\n    successor test already checks evidence per rule and correlates each completion to its\\n    instance by the scheduled event id it references, so the retired assertion's cause may be gone.\\n  - (3) The recorder closes INCONCLUSIVE on two paths. (a) Run INCOMPLETE: an execution error, for\\n    example a declared 5000 ms instruction timeout (`finish-workflow`, `respond-async`) running out\\n    under load. (b) Run COMPLETED with an unresolved rule: the correlated monitor answers\\n    INCONCLUSIVE when an obligation is still pending or when not every processed evidence item was\\n    accepted. The async Case completes the operation from the controller as soon as the handler's\\n    completion authority exists, which can be before the server records the start. The server then\\n    takes the completion-before-start path (upstream temporal#6821): it synthesizes the started\\n    event, dated with the start time the completion carries, and that date can precede evidence the\\n    recorder has already observed. CHASM does this when it handles the completion; the hsm\\n    implementation fabricates the missing started event on its own path. Both switch values must\\n    be confirmed to produce the same history shape before a Model admits it.\\n\\n## API Contracts\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n- **Harness invocation.** Inputs: a test selection regex, an iteration count, a mode (`process`\\n  or `in-process`), and a record file. Output is one line per iteration, `<iteration> PASS|FAIL\\n  <signature-hash>...` (one hash per failing leaf test in that iteration, none on PASS). Then one\\n  line per test identity, `<test> <failed>/<ran>`, and one line per distinct signature,\\n  `<signature-hash> <count>/<ran>`, with the test identity and the first failing assertion. The\\n  exit status is 0 only when every iteration passed. In `in-process` mode an iteration is one\\n  repeat of the selection inside the single process, numbered by the order of the tests' start\\n  events, because repeats share a test name.\\n- **Record file.** Each finished iteration appends one JSON line: `{iteration, mode, selection,\\n  commit, fingerprint, outcome, failures:[{test, signature_hash, signature}], runs:[path]}`. A\\n  summary can be printed from one or more record files, so a loop split across sessions adds up to\\n  one count; files with different fingerprints are never summed.\\n- **Run capture.** When `UMPIRE_REPEAT_RUN_DIR` names a directory, each affected test writes every\\n  Run it closes there as a recorded Run file (the format the replay package already writes), and\\n  the umpire-run test passes the CLI's existing `--record` flag. Unset, nothing is written.\\n- **Signature line.** A failing live assertion prints exactly one line `TESTPILOT-SIGNATURE <json>`,\\n  where `<json>` is the failure signature below on one line. When one test prints several, the\\n  first one counts.\\n- **Failure signature (canonical order).** `{test, assertion, run_disposition, verdict_status,\\n  unresolved_rules:[{rule_id,status,terminal_state_id}], diagnostics:[{kind,code}], leaks:[line]}`.\\n  Iteration-specific values such as Run ids, namespace suffixes, timestamps, ports, addresses and\\n  durations are normalized out, and lists are sorted, so repeated occurrences of one cause hash\\n  equal. The assertion is hashed without its source line number, so an edit that moves a line does\\n  not split one cause into two signatures; the record keeps the location for reading. Two reserved\\n  signatures carry only `test` and `assertion`: `unparsed` (a failure with no readable signature\\n  line and no readable assertion) and `process` (the test process ended without a pass or fail\\n  event for a test that started: a crash, a kill or a test-binary timeout; the assertion is the\\n  last output line).\\n- **Quarantine entry.** Allowed only under R7. It holds exactly: `{test, signature, issue_url,\\n  max_retries: 1, allowed_outcomes: [INCONCLUSIVE, INCOMPLETE, <named infrastructure error>]}`,\\n  where `signature` is the canonical failure signature itself, compared field by field with the\\n  one the live test builds. The live test never recomputes the harness's hash. Nothing else is\\n  added.\\n\\n## Edge Cases & Constraints\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n- **SEM-16.** Runtime code must not add implicit retry. Any retry lives in the live test and never\\n  inside Testpilot, the Driver or the evaluator.\\n- **QLF-05.** A proved VIOLATED Verdict stays violated. No retry, re-evaluation or quarantine may\\n  turn a VIOLATED attempt into a pass, and no fix may weaken the evaluator (pending-to-satisfied,\\n  dropping an obligation, widening a window) to hide INCONCLUSIVE.\\n- **Case authority.** Instruction timeouts and Contract deadlines belong to the Case. A budget that\\n  is too tight is fixed in the Model or Producer that derives it, and the fixture is regenerated.\\n  It is never overridden at runtime or in the test.\\n- **The fn-83 CLI contract stays unchanged.** With `--create`, umpire-run deletes both the namespace\\n  and the endpoint on exit, and prints one stderr line per resource it could not remove.\\n- **Failure rates.** The measured rates are low, around 10% per affected test per suite run, and the\\n  in-process mode raises them. A handful of runs therefore cannot settle anything; see R1 and R8.\\n- **Retired identities.** A failure whose original test was retired may have gone with it (the\\n  retired test's fixed-order assertion is gone). That outcome is a measured result, not an\\n  assumption.\\n- **Live suite cost.** The live suite takes about 30 minutes, and other agents share the host. The\\n  harness must allow a narrow selection. The R2 and R8 loops take hours of wall-clock time, and one\\n  caller-test iteration starts one cluster per switch value. The record file lets a loop stop and\\n  resume across sessions without losing counts.\\n- **Shared host.** Load from other sessions changes timing, and path (3a) depends on timing. Every\\n  loop records the commit, the host's load average and the number of other `go test` processes\\n  when it starts. Before and after rates are compared only under similar load.\\n- **Measured tree.** Loops run from a commit, with no uncommitted edits in what the live tests\\n  build or read (`tests/`, `common/testing/testpilot/`, `tools/umpire/`, the Case fixtures and the\\n  Lean helper binaries), because other sessions edit this working tree. The per-iteration\\n  fingerprint (Architecture) catches an edit that lands during a loop.\\n- **fn-88 overlap.** fn-88 (in progress in another session) pins checker state counts on the\\n  Nexus Caller and Pair Models. A fix under R5 or R6 that changes either Model, or the Nexus\\n  realization, must not land while fn-88 is open unless it is coordinated with that session;\\n  fn-88 then re-pins its counts.\\n\\n## Acceptance Criteria\\n<!-- scope: both -->\\n\\n- **R1:** A reproduction harness implements the Harness, Record file and Signature line contracts\\n  in both modes and prints per-identity and per-signature failure rates. Errors: a build failure\\n  or a selection matching no test exits non-zero and counts zero iterations, never passes (Go\\n  reports an unmatched `-run` as a passing package, so the harness checks that a selected test\\n  started); a failure the signature parser cannot read is reported as the `unparsed` signature,\\n  never merged into a known one; an interrupted loop reports and records the iterations it\\n  completed and does not count the interrupted one; a changed input fingerprint stops the loop the\\n  same way and names what changed; a malformed record-file line, or record files with different\\n  fingerprints, fail the summary and name the file and line.\\n- **R2:** Each failure is re-measured on its current identities, at a recorded commit that\\n  precedes any R4 to R6 fix. (1) `TestTestpilotUmpireRunRunsACheckedInCaseAgainstAnyEndpoint`\\n  runs at least 50 process-mode iterations; failures of kind (3) in this test are counted from\\n  the same loop under their own signatures. (2) `TestTestpilotNexusPairCase` runs at least 200\\n  iterations. (3) `TestTestpilotNexusCallerAsyncCompletion`,\\n  `TestTestpilotNexusCallerCaseRunsFromItsFixtureNameAlone` and\\n  `TestTestpilotWorkerOutageCaseLeavesAnotherQueueAlone` run at least 200 iterations each, plus at\\n  least 50 in-process iterations of `-count=4` over the first two. A loop may stop early for an\\n  identity once one signature has 5 occurrences there; the rate is then reported with its 95%\\n  interval. The rates, intervals, signatures, commit and host load are recorded in the task\\n  receipt. Errors: a failure with zero occurrences across the full count closes as \\\"not\\n  reproduced on the successor\\\"; that record names the retired identity, its successor and the\\n  iteration count, and it never claims a root cause. A signature outside (1) to (3) is recorded\\n  and left out of scope (see Boundaries).\\n- **R3:** The affected tests' disposition, Verdict and rule-status assertions print the Signature\\n  line (Run disposition, diagnostics, and each unresolved rule's status and terminal state), in\\n  `TestTestpilotNexusPairCase`, the Nexus caller Query tests, both worker-outage tests and the\\n  umpire-run test. For the umpire-run test, whose Run lives in a child process, the CLI's report\\n  also prints the Run's diagnostics, and the test's failure message carries the process exit\\n  status, whether it was killed, and the elapsed time. One failing run then tells path (3a) from\\n  path (3b), an ordering mismatch from a Contract violation, and a teardown kill from an output\\n  mismatch. The same tests implement Run capture (API Contracts). The part that builds a signature\\n  from a Run and Verdict compiles without the `integration` tag, so an offline unit test pins it and\\n  an R7 quarantine can reuse it. Errors: no error surface beyond test failure output; with Run\\n  capture unset nothing is written, and a capture directory that cannot be written fails the test\\n  naming the path; the CLI's exit codes and stderr leak lines are unchanged.\\n- **R4:** Resolve (1) at its cause. The umpire-run live test runs against a cluster where the\\n  deletion that `--create` issues can finish. The preferred way is a test cluster with the system\\n  worker service. The test then asserts that the endpoint is gone and that describing the\\n  namespace by its original name answers not-found within a bounded wait, and its run time no\\n  longer includes a 30 s teardown timeout. This fix is made whether or not R2 reproduces (1),\\n  because every run of the test today pays the stuck delete. Errors: a namespace or endpoint that\\n  is still present at the end of the wait fails the test and names the resource. A deletion that\\n  exhausts the teardown budget still prints its stderr leak line and fails the test. The\\n  unreachable-endpoint test keeps exit 3.\\n- **R5:** Resolve (2) at its cause. Every live assertion over the pair Case correlates each\\n  instance's evidence by its key (the scheduled event id a completion references), never by\\n  global position. If R2 or R3 shows that the pair Case's Scenario or Contract admits only one\\n  completion order that the server does not guarantee, the Model or Producer is fixed so the Case\\n  admits every order the server may record, and the fixture is regenerated. Errors: a missing\\n  completion still yields INCONCLUSIVE. When R5 changes the pair Model, Producer or Contract, a\\n  live control proves that a completion referencing the other instance's scheduled event still\\n  yields VIOLATED, and the existing single-instance forged-completion control keeps passing.\\n- **R6:** Resolve (3) at the cause R3 names. On path (3a), the Model's declared bound is corrected\\n  and the fixture regenerated, and the receipt states the latency the new bound covers: the p99 of\\n  the bounded step over the R2 iterations, taken from the Runs that Run capture wrote (passing and\\n  failing), with the margin stated. On path (3b), either the async Model admits the server's completion-before-start path\\n  (the synthesized started event) as the product behavior it is, or the Program orders the\\n  completion after the start is observed; the Decision Context records which, and why. Errors: a\\n  Run that is truly incomplete (worker stopped, deadline passed) still closes INCONCLUSIVE or\\n  INCOMPLETE; a completion violating the Contract still closes VIOLATED; hsm and chasm must still\\n  agree (a Verdict that differs between the switch values still fails, naming both).\\n- **R7:** A signature may be quarantined only when R3 evidence shows its cause is outside this\\n  repository's control, for example a server or SDK race with an upstream issue. The quarantine\\n  entry must match the API contract, and its retry must live in the live test, not the runtime. The\\n  retry fires only when the failing attempt's signature equals the entry's signature field by field\\n  and its outcome is in `allowed_outcomes`, at most once, and every retry is logged with the first\\n  attempt's signature. The retry decision compiles without the `integration` tag. Errors: any attempt with a VIOLATED Verdict fails immediately with no retry; a\\n  signature that does not match fails with no retry; an entry with no issue link or with\\n  `max_retries` above 1 is rejected by an offline unit test that also proves the first two\\n  errors. With no quarantine entry, no retry code exists.\\n- **R8:** Definition of done: every signature from (1) to (3) is resolved, closed as not\\n  reproduced, or quarantined, and these all pass on the final commit. First, the R2 iteration\\n  counts again, per identity, with zero failures and no early stop (with 200 iterations the 95%\\n  upper bound on the per-test failure rate is 1.5%; with 50 it is 6%). Second, five consecutive\\n  `make umpire-check-live-tests` runs exit 0 with no retry logged, unless a quarantine is logged\\n  and linked. Third, `make umpire-check-regression` once. The receipt carries the per-signature\\n  rates before and after, compared by signature class, and the host load of each loop. Errors:\\n  one failure of an identity resets that identity's count only; a gate run that fails on any\\n  identity in (1) to (3), or on a test this spec changed, restarts the five-run sequence. A gate run\\n  whose only failures are identities outside that set (the canary harness tests, for example)\\n  neither counts toward the five nor resets them; its signatures are recorded in the receipt and\\n  listed as a follow-up in `UMPIRE4_ORDER.md`. A new signature found during the loops is triaged\\n  under R2 and is never retried away.\\n- **R9:** On close, the `UMPIRE4_ORDER.md` queue item for this spec is replaced by an entry in the\\n  delivered list, the gate baselines record the after rates, any quarantine is listed with its\\n  issue link, and the Testpilot provisioning notes say that a `--create` namespace deletion\\n  finishes only on a cluster that runs the system worker. No error surface beyond doc review.\\n\\n## Boundaries\\n<!-- scope: business -->\\n\\n- Out of scope: runtime retry in Testpilot, the Driver or the evaluator (SEM-16), and any change\\n  that turns a pending rule into a satisfied one.\\n- Out of scope: changes to server Nexus or namespace-deletion behavior. A server race found here\\n  gets an upstream issue, not a server patch.\\n- Out of scope: a suite-wide retry, `-failfast` changes, or a non-empty expected-failure set in\\n  `umpire-check-live-tests`.\\n- Out of scope: failures outside (1) to (3), such as the canary harness tests and the\\n  `context canceled` noise at cluster shutdown. They are only recorded.\\n- Out of scope: running the harness in CI or on a schedule.\\n- Out of scope: the \\\"one Contract monitor per entity\\\" rework.\\n\\n## Decision Context\\n<!-- scope: both -->\\n\\nRoot cause first, because every workaround is costly. A retry or a looser assertion can mask the\\nregression the gate exists to catch, and both SEM-16 and QLF-05 forbid the forms that could turn a\\nviolation into a pass. A quarantine is the last resort, and it is scoped to one exact signature so\\nthat it cannot absorb a new failure.\\n\\nFor (1), running the test cluster with the system worker is preferred over two alternatives:\\nsetting `RetainNamespace` from the test, or adding a hidden CLI flag. Both would stop exercising\\nthe `--create` deletion that fn-83 promises, and the flag would change the CLI contract. The worker\\nservice costs one dedicated cluster, which this test already has, and it should remove the fixed\\n30 s teardown timeout from its run time.\\n\\nThe gate target follows from the rates. At about 15% red suite runs, five green suite runs alone\\nhappen about 45% of the time with no fix at all. The per-identity loops carry the statistical\\nweight, and the suite runs show the fixes compose. Re-measuring comes first because the\\nidentities changed under fn-86. Resolving a failure that no longer exists would waste the spec,\\nand so would assuming it is gone.\\n\\nThe fix tasks for (2) and (3) are conditional on the measurement. Each one starts from the\\nbaseline receipt and may close as \\\"not reproduced on the successor\\\" with that evidence, without a\\ncode change beyond confirming its R-ID's test-side clause. The fix for (1) is not conditional: the\\nstuck delete is certain on every run, whatever the measured failure rate.\\n\\nThe harness reads `go test -json` events and a one-line signature the tests print, rather than\\nparsing assertion text. Assertion text changes with every message edit, while the signature fields\\nare the facts that tell the causes apart. It is a Go command beside the other Umpire developer\\ncommands, not a shell loop: it needs the event parsing, the normalization and a unit-tested\\nsignature hash. The repository's CI test runner re-runs every failed test blindly, so it is the\\npattern this spec avoids, not one to reuse.\\n\\nRejected as overkill: sharing the signature type between the harness and the live tests as Go\\ncode. The one-line JSON format is the whole interface, and a shared package would couple the\\nintegration-tagged tests to the tool's build.\\n\\nA loop detects a changed tree instead of running from a frozen copy. A `git archive` export would\\nhave no built Lean helper binaries and would need a cold model build per commit, and worktrees are\\nnot used in this repository. The fingerprint costs one hash of a few directories per iteration and\\nturns a silent mix of versions into a stopped loop.\\n\\nA red gate run caused only by an out-of-scope test does not count as green, and it does not reset\\nthe five either. Counting it green would hide a failure; resetting on it would make this spec's\\ndone depend on flakes it leaves out of scope, and invite re-running until five green runs line up.\\nRecording it and listing it as a follow-up keeps it visible.\\n\\n## Quick commands\\n\\n```bash\\n# Offline: the harness and its signature parser\\ngo test -tags test_dep ./tools/umpire/cmd/umpire-repeat/...\\n# Compile the live tests without a cluster\\ngo vet -tags 'test_dep integration' ./tests\\n# A narrow live loop\\nmake umpire-repeat-run SELECT='^TestTestpilotNexusPairCase$' COUNT=5 MODE=process RECORD=/path/to/record.jsonl\\n# The gate\\nmake umpire-check-live-tests\\n```\\n\\n## Early proof point\\n\\nTask fn-90-resolve-the-intermittent-live-testpilot.3 validates the approach: it shows which of the\\nthree failures still occur on today's identities, and with which signatures. Lean renders canonical ProtoJSON, six fixtures are committed under `tests/testcore/testpilot/testdata`, and `umpire-check-case-runtime-conformance` fails on drift. |\\n| Same model for local, CI, canary | Half done. Unchanged Case bytes bind to two physical environments through `DeriveProfile`. No consumer other than the functional tests exists; the canary is deferred (fn-70, fn-29). |\\n| White-box and black-box modes | Black-box structurally: the Driver reaches the server only over gRPC and HTTP and holds no in-process handle. Nothing runs a Case against an endpoint outside the functional test cluster, and no example shows an assertion that needs white-box access. |\\n| Developer-friendly API | The five-command Model syntax is friendly. Everything after it is not. The Producer is raw Lean; the typed examples use no syntax at all (550 and 940 lines with proof terms); adding the worker-outage Case touched 14 files and 585 lines; two hand-maintained registries name every Case; the only tutorial documents keywords that do not exist. |\\n| Exploration mode | Lean-only. `Umpire.Exploration` is a complete pure engine whose output is a Plan, and no path turns a Plan into a Case. `umpire-fuzz` does not exist. |\\n| Faults as first-class citizens | One fault pair (worker stop, worker resume) works end to end on the real Driver. It is declared in raw Lean beside a hand-written Program, not in the Model syntax. |\\n| Non-linear steps (IDs unknown until runtime) | Done. Slots, response projections, `RunRef`, Contract captures, and correlated captures exist and are live. |\\n| Pre-programmed SDK workers | Done for the shapes in use. Workflow and Nexus-handler entrypoints are interpreted inside a real Go SDK worker. Activity entrypoints are validated but have no interpreter; signals, timers, child workflows and continue-as-new are absent from the instruction set. |\\n| Guided exploration, guided fuzzing | Absent beyond the offline guided selector. |\\n| Clock skew across processes | Partial. Deadlines are logical (`rule_events`, operation transitions) or one host's milliseconds. |\\n| Translate existing functional tests | Absent. No test in the tree is a translation, and no author outside the Umpire maintainers has written one. |\\n\\nOf the vision's seven acceptance criteria, three are met (Go SDK driven, one fault injected,\\ndeterministic file artifacts), one is half met (two consumers), and three are unmet (explorative\\ntest, translated functional test, syntax friendly enough for Lean newbies past the Model block).\\n\\n### What this spec does\\n\\nThe single largest distance from the vision is that **a developer who can write the Model cannot\\nget a running test without also writing a Producer**. Every other unmet item either depends on\\nclosing that gap or is already owned by an open spec. Exploration (fn-33) cannot emit Cases\\nbecause there is no generic Producer; the canary (fn-70) needs nothing new from authoring; clock\\nskew and guided fuzzing have no consumer yet.\\n\\nSo this spec makes one thing true: **a Model file plus one `case` block is a running, checked-in,\\ndeterministic live test, and a fault is one line in a Scenario.** It measures that claim by\\ntranslating one upstream functional test using nothing but the command syntax, and by\\nre-authoring the worker-outage Case as a Model with two fault lines. The translation also\\nsupplies the vision's white-box example: one upstream assertion reads mutable state through the\\nadmin service, and the translation records it as a Known Gap that no black-box Run can close.\\n\\nIt deliberately adds no exploration, no canary consumer, no Program-level surface syntax, no new\\nfault or instruction kinds, and no clock model. Boundaries names the owning spec for each.\\n\\n## Architecture & Data Models\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n### Ownership\\n\\n```text\\nModel file (command syntax)                     model/Temporal/Feature/<Feature>/<Name>.lean\\n  model / property / scenario [fault] / limits / query / case\\n        |\\n        v  elaborates into\\nChecked Model + selected witness                 Umpire.Model, Umpire.Search (existing)\\n        |\\n        v  Producer, written once                Umpire.Case.Producer   (new, SCP-02 clean)\\nRealization template + evidence + faults         Temporal.Case.Template.* (new, Temporal-owned)\\n        |\\n        v  compile                               Umpire.Case.Compiler   (existing)\\nCase bytes                                       umpire-case --list / --render\\n        |\\n        v  one generator, one functional table   umpire-gen-case-runtime-conformance\\ntests/testcore/testpilot/testdata/<name>-case.json\\n        |\\n        v  one live test per Case                tests/testpilot_<name>_case_test.go\\n```\\n\\nThe Producer is written once. It reads the checked witness trace, the Scenario's Action order,\\nthe Property's clauses, and the `case` block's evidence mapping, and builds the Program from a\\nnamed realization template and the Contract by the derivation fn-80 R1 proved for the Nexus\\nsuccess Case. `Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Success.Producer` is deleted; its generic half becomes\\n`Umpire.Case.Producer` and its Program becomes one template.\\n\\n### R1 the `case` block and the generic Producer\\n\\nA sixth command joins the five fn-82 respells. It names a `find` Query, a realization template\\nwith its parameters, and the history event that confirms each Action:\\n\\n```lean\\ncase asyncNexusSuccess fixture \\\"async-nexus\\\"\\n  realizes completion\\n  as nexusOperation service \\\"umpire.case.service\\\" operation \\\"complete\\\" responds async\\n  evidence\\n    awaitStart   \\u2190 history nexusOperationStarted\\n    awaitSuccess \\u2190 history nexusOperationCompleted\\n```\\n\\n`fixture` names the checked-in file and derives every identity the Case carries: the Case ID is\\n`temporal.case.<fixture>`, the Program ID `<caseId>.program`, the Contract ID `<caseId>.contract`,\\nand the run-scope literal `<fixture>`. The grammar has no other identity slot. `realizes` takes a\\n`find` Query; a `verify` Query rejects with a located error, because a Case realizes one selected\\ntrace. `as` names a template and its parameters. `evidence` maps each Action the Scenario selects\\nto one recorded history event kind. The Step each event confirms (state, outcome, facts) is read\\nfrom the checked Machine along the witness trace, so the author writes it once, in the `steps`\\nblock. Hook names for fault lines are resolved here too, because only the `case` block knows the\\ntemplate. The `case` command is an elaborator, not a macro: it resolves names and registers the\\nCase (R4).\\n\\nDefinition IDs and sources are per file. The command syntax derives the definition family from\\nthe enclosing namespace and the source location from the elaborating file, so two Models that\\nboth name a `lifecycle` in different files carry distinct Definition IDs and distinct Provenance\\nsources. The Nexus success family keeps its current value under that derivation.\\n\\n`Umpire.Case.Producer` is the reusable half. Its input is an Umpire-owned record: the checked\\nModel, its vocabulary as `ModelValue` lists, the checked Property, the checked Scenario, the\\nwitness trace, and the Query's ID, source, and Known Gaps; the Temporal authoring bundle converts\\nto it at the call site, because MOD-01 forbids the Producer from importing a Temporal type. With\\nit come an abstract `Realization`, an evidence mapping, and fault lines. Its output is the\\n`Umpire.Case.Compiler.Input` the Nexus success Producer builds by hand today. Nothing in it names\\nTemporal (SCP-02); the Temporal-named constants move to the templates. The Correlated-clause\\nderivation, the same-step vacuity rejection, the coverage check that every Property clause\\nappears in the Contract, and the Provenance bindings move unchanged. Witness selection is\\ndeterministic under PLN-02, which is what makes the fixture stable.\\n\\nProof of R1 in two steps. The extraction alone (Realization built by hand with today's IDs)\\nregenerates the async-Nexus fixture byte-identical. The `case` block then regenerates it with the\\nderived identities: the diff is limited to the Case ID, Program ID, Contract ID, run-scope literal,\\nand Provenance; with those fields masked the Program and Contract are byte-identical, the receipt\\nlists the diff, and the live async-Nexus tests pass with no change beyond the fixture name.\\n\\n### R2 realization templates\\n\\n`Temporal.Case.Template` owns the Temporal-specific Programs. Two templates cover every shape the\\nModel-bearing and fault-bearing checked-in Cases use:\\n\\n- `nexusOperation service operation responds sync|async`: a controller-started workflow\\n  schedules one Nexus operation on the Case's endpoint role; the handler responds synchronously,\\n  or asynchronously with the controller completing it; the controller reads history. The `async`\\n  form is today's async-Nexus Program verbatim. In the `sync` form nothing sequences the history\\n  read after completion, so the template first reads with the close-event filter, as the\\n  worker-outage Program does, and only then performs the full read the Projection consumes.\\n  Hooks: `start`, `completion`.\\n- `workflow type`: a controller-started workflow that finishes; the controller reads history. This\\n  is today's worker-outage Program without its two fault instructions. Hooks: `start`,\\n  `completion`. Its fault rule ID stays `worker-outage-order` so the existing artifact assertion\\n  keeps its meaning.\\n\\nA template is a Lean value, not syntax; adding a third is one Lean file. The template owns the\\nevidence sources the Correlated capability reads, because they are Temporal-specific and depend\\non which event kinds the `case` block maps: for each admitted event kind the template states the\\nattributes field, the operation-key path, the evidence kind ID, the source ID, and the scope\\nliteral, and its Program is a function of the resolved evidence rules, so the history node's\\nprojection targets are built from the mapping rather than patched afterwards. The Nexus template\\nkeys operations by the scheduled event ID as today; the `workflow` template runs one workflow per\\nRun and keys by the run-scope literal, because a completed-workflow event names no operation. The\\n`evidence` block's `history <eventKind>` resolves against the generated history event attribute\\noneof in `Temporal.API`, so a misspelled kind rejects with the admitted kinds listed, the same way\\nan unknown Action does today. This resolution reads generated names the author never declared,\\nwhich AUT-09 as drafted does not cover; see Parked unknowns.\\n\\nThe typed examples (`TypedUnary`, `TypedNexus`) stay on the expert path unchanged.\\n\\n### R3 faults in the Scenario\\n\\nA Scenario may carry fault lines:\\n\\n```lean\\nscenario completionSurvivesOutage on lifecycle\\n  operation starts pending\\n  actions exactly [completion: awaitCompletion]\\n  fault workerStop before start\\n  fault workerResume after start\\n```\\n\\n`fault <kind> before|after <hook>` names one of the two existing fault kinds and one hook of the\\ntemplate the `case` block chooses. The Producer owns the hook-to-instruction map the fault\\nlowering has always left to its caller: it lowers each line through the existing intent and\\nrealization types in `Umpire.Variations`, with the template's task-queue role as the outage role;\\nthe lowering itself keeps ignoring the occurrence. Ordering in a controller entrypoint comes only\\nfrom dependency edges, and a realization can only make the fault depend on something, so the\\nProducer also rewrites edges: for `before`, the hook's instruction gains a dependency and guard\\non the fault; for `after`, the hook's successors gain a dependency on the fault. That is what\\nmakes a stop precede the start it names, as today's hand-written Program does. A hook the\\ntemplate does not declare rejects by name at the `fault` line, listing the template's hooks. Two\\nfault lines with the same kind, hook and placement reject as duplicates. Stop and resume pairing\\nis not checked at elaboration; the outage-order rule the Producer adds to the Contract whenever a\\nScenario carries faults (fn-80 R4, a `rule_events` Deadline over the recorded `FAULT_INJECTED`\\nevents in declared order) answers an unpaired stop at Run time.\\n\\nProof of R3: the worker-outage Case file is deleted and re-authored as a Model (`pending \\u2192\\ncompleted` on `awaitCompletion`, one Fact `completed`), a Property requiring the completed state\\nand fact, the Scenario above, and a `case` block on the `workflow` template. The fixture changes\\nbytes: its Provenance carries Model bindings, and its completion requirement is now Correlated\\nclauses rather than the hand-written safety rule, so the Verdict carries the outage-order rule\\n(same ID, terminal state `resumed`) plus one satisfied rule per clause. Both live outage tests\\npass against that shape, and the offline artifact test pins the new bytes and the `rule_events`\\nDeadline.\\n\\n### R4 one registry, two authored files per Case\\n\\nThe `case` command registers its Case in a Lean environment extension that stores the\\ndeclaration name of the Case value and the fixture name; a persistent extension lives in the\\n`.olean`, so it holds names, never closures. A term elaborator in the renderer's root module\\nmaterializes the registered names into the sorted list the executable enumerates, and the\\nduplicate-fixture diagnostic fires there. The two typed examples register their existing Case\\nvalues with an explicit call so `--list` covers every functional fixture; their Programs,\\nProfiles, and Contracts do not change. The synthetic and conformance Cases do not register: the\\nGo conformance builder keeps naming them by renderer argument, because its entries carry expected\\nVerdicts the registry does not model. `umpire-case --list` prints every registered Case as\\n`<case-id> <fixture-name>` sorted by Case ID; `--render <id>` prints its canonical bytes. A Model\\nfile the aggregator module does not import registers nothing, so its fixture is absent and its\\nlive test fails at load; that failure is loud enough that no lint is added. The Go generator's\\nhand-written functional table and its fixed-count guard are deleted; it calls `--list` and\\nrenders each entry. The Lean renderer's functional dispatch table is deleted.\\n\\nThe offline artifact tests split by kind. One table-driven test over the testdata directory\\ndecodes every fixture, prepares it against its derived Profile, and pins its identity; the\\nper-Case semantic assertions (Deadline units, tenfold load, run isolation, typed Profiles) stay\\nas small per-Case functions. `runCase` derives a default binding (namespace, task queue, Nexus\\nendpoint) from the fixture name so a live test passes only the name; tests that vary bindings\\nstill pass one. The evidence helpers the async-Nexus test defines move to the shared live-test\\nhelper file.\\n\\nAdding a Case after this spec touches exactly two authored files: the Model `.lean` file (its\\n`inductive` declarations included) and one live Go test whose body is the `runCase` call and\\nVerdict assertions. The fixture JSON is generated. R6's commit is the measurement.\\n\\n### R5 the authoring walkthrough\\n\\n`model/AUTHORING.md` replaces the Nexus success tutorial. It walks from an empty file to a green\\nlive test: the `inductive` declarations, the five blocks, the `case` block,\\n`make umpire-gen-case-runtime-conformance`, the Go test, and `make umpire-check-regression`. It\\nlists every located diagnostic an author can hit, each with the fix. Every Lean code block in it\\nis the literal content of a region of the R6 Model file delimited by `-- authoring: <name>`\\nmarker comments, and a Go test under `tools/umpire` asserts that equality, so the tutorial cannot\\ndrift again. The old tutorial's \\\"proposed, not implemented\\\" forms move verbatim to a `DESIGN.md`\\nbeside the Nexus success Model. The integration note beside it is reduced to the ID convention\\nand the Case boundary.\\n\\n### R6 translate one upstream functional test\\n\\nThe upstream Nexus workflow suite's synchronous completion test (`TestNexusOperationSyncCompletion`)\\nis re-authored as a Model file using only the six commands and its `inductive` declarations, with\\nno new template and no Producer code. It exercises the `nexusOperation` template's `sync` form.\\nA `COVERAGE.md` beside the Model maps each upstream assertion to a Property clause, a Contract\\nrule, or a Known Gap in the Case's Provenance: the result value and the completed history event\\nare carried; the handler-supplied link on the completed event is a Known Gap unless the template\\ncan express it; the admin-service mutable-state assertion is a Known Gap of kind white-box, which\\nis the vision's example of a test a black-box consumer cannot run. It also records that the\\nupstream handler is an external Nexus server while the translation's handler runs inside the\\nTestpilot worker. The upstream test is not deleted.\\n\\n### R7 run a Case against any endpoint\\n\\nProvisioning leaves the functional test file. `common/testing/testpilot/temporal/provision`\\nregisters a namespace and polls its description until the namespace cache serves it, creates a\\nNexus endpoint, and deletes both through the operator service, over the operator and\\nworkflow-service gRPC clients only; it takes no test environment, no `testing.T`, and no\\nserver-internal namespace package, which is the seam the fn-70 deferral note recorded as the\\nreason the helpers stayed test-local. The live tests call it. Transport credentials are the\\ninsecure ones the live tests use; TLS and authentication flags are out of scope.\\n\\n`tools/umpire/cmd/umpire-run` takes a fixture path, a gRPC address, an HTTP address, a namespace,\\na task queue, an optional Nexus endpoint name, `--create`, and `--timeout`. It derives the Profile\\nthrough `DeriveProfile`, builds the composite Driver with its own SDK worker, runs once, and prints\\nthe Run status, cleanup status, Verdict status, and one line per rule Verdict. Exit codes: 0\\nsatisfied, 1 violated, 2 inconclusive, 3 preparation, infrastructure, or Run error. Without\\n`--create` the resources must exist and are never deleted; with it an existing namespace or\\nendpoint is an error, and both are deleted on exit. SIGINT and the timeout cancel the Run and\\ntear down best-effort, printing one stderr line per resource that could not be removed. Only\\nCases whose Profile `DeriveProfile` derives are runnable (three of the six checked-in fixtures\\ntoday); a typed fixture rejects with the `PreparationError` category on stderr. The CLI takes no\\nlock; concurrent invocations need caller-unique names. It imports the Driver and the SDK, never\\n`tests/testcore` or `service/`.\\n\\nProof of R7: a tagged test starts the functional cluster, runs the binary as a subprocess against\\nits addresses with the async-Nexus fixture, and asserts exit 0. This is the vision's black-box\\nmode on real bytes, and the seam fn-70 and fn-29 consume.\\n\\n## API Contracts\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n```lean\\nnamespace Umpire.Case\\n\\n/-- The checked authoring bundle, Umpire-owned; the Temporal authoring bundle converts to it. -/\\nstructure Producer.Input where\\n  model : CheckedModel\\n  vocabulary : Vocabulary            -- states, actions, outcomes, facts as ModelValue lists\\n  property : CheckedProperty\\n  scenario : CheckedScenario\\n  witness : Trace\\n  queryId : DefinitionId\\n  querySource : SourceLocation\\n  knownGaps : KnownGaps\\n\\nstructure Identity where\\n  caseId : String                    -- temporal.case.<fixture>; program and contract IDs derive\\n  fixture : String\\n\\ninductive HookPlacement | before | after\\nstructure Hook where\\n  name : String\\n  instruction : InstructionRef\\n\\n/-- What the template knows about one admitted event kind. -/\\nstructure EvidenceSource where\\n  eventKind : String\\n  attributesField : String\\n  operationKeyPath : Path\\n  kindId : DefinitionId\\n  sourceId : DefinitionId\\n\\nstructure EvidenceRule where           -- one resolved (action, source) pair\\n  action : ModelValue\\n  source : EvidenceSource\\n\\nstructure Realization where\\n  roles : Array RoleDefinition\\n  environment : Array EnvironmentDefinition\\n  program : Identity \\u2192 List EvidenceRule \\u2192 Program\\n  historyObservation : String\\n  operationKey : DefinitionId\\n  scopeField : DefinitionId\\n  taskQueueRole : String\\n  faultRuleId : String\\n  hooks : List Hook\\n  sources : List EvidenceSource\\n\\nstructure EvidenceMapping where\\n  action : ModelValue\\n  eventKind : String\\n\\nstructure FaultLine where\\n  kind : FaultKind            -- .workerStop | .workerResume\\n  hook : String\\n  placement : HookPlacement\\n\\ndef Producer.produce\\n    (input : Producer.Input) (identity : Identity) (realization : Realization)\\n    (evidence : List EvidenceMapping) (faults : List FaultLine)\\n    (required : List DefinitionId := []) :\\n    Except LoweringError testpilot.v1.Case\\n\\nend Umpire.Case\\n```\\n\\n```lean\\nnamespace Temporal.Case.Template\\ninductive Response | sync | async\\ndef nexusOperation (service operation : String) (responds : Response) : Umpire.Case.Realization\\ndef workflow (workflowType : String) : Umpire.Case.Realization\\nend Temporal.Case.Template\\n```\\n\\nCommand syntax additions (exact grammar is a task decision; these forms are normative):\\n\\n```text\\ncase <name> fixture \\\"<fixture-name>\\\"\\n  realizes <query>\\n  as <template> <param>*\\n  evidence\\n    (<action> \\u2190 history <eventKind>)+\\n\\nscenario ... (fault <workerStop|workerResume> <before|after> <hook>)*\\n```\\n\\nThe registry stores `(declaration name, case ID, fixture name)` per registered Case; the\\nrenderer's root module materializes the sorted list through a term elaborator.\\n\\n```text\\numpire-case --list                   # <case-id> <fixture-name>, sorted by case-id\\numpire-case --render <case-id>       # canonical ProtoJSON on stdout\\n```\\n\\n```text\\numpire-run --case <path> --grpc <addr> --http <addr> --namespace <ns> --task-queue <q>\\n           [--nexus-endpoint <name>] [--create] [--timeout <duration>]\\nexit 0 satisfied | 1 violated | 2 inconclusive | 3 preparation, infrastructure, or Run error\\n```\\n\\nProto: no field-number changes, no new messages, no new fault kinds, no new instruction kinds.\\n\\n## Edge Cases & Constraints\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\n- A `case` whose Query is `verify` rejects at elaboration naming the Query. A `find` Query whose\\n  Search returns no witness rejects at production.\\n- An `evidence` line for an Action the Scenario never selects, or a selected Action with no\\n  evidence line, rejects by Action name listing the selected Actions. An event kind the generated\\n  API does not know rejects listing the admitted kinds.\\n- A `fault` line on a Scenario no `case` block realizes is never elaborated and needs no\\n  diagnostic; one whose hook the chosen template lacks rejects at the `fault` line.\\n- Two `case` blocks with the same fixture name reject at registration.\\n- The Producer keeps fn-80's vacuity rule: a `require` whose response holds earlier in the witness\\n  trace than its Action rejects as `property.clause-early-response`. R6 must not weaken it; an\\n  assertion that needs to is a Known Gap.\\n- Hand-editing a fixture remains forbidden (ART-11, ART-12); every byte change goes through the\\n  owning target and is explained in the task receipt.\\n- `umpire-run` never embeds addresses or credentials in a Case (QLF-01) and never broadens Limits\\n  (CLI-02).\\n- The generic Producer stays under SCP-02: no Temporal name, history attribute name, or role ID\\n  string. Those live in `Temporal.Case.Template`.\\n\\n## Quick commands\\n\\n```sh\\ncd model && lake build                          # elaborate every Model and case block\\nmake umpire-gen-case-runtime-conformance        # regenerate every registered fixture\\nmake umpire-check-case-runtime-conformance      # fail on fixture drift\\nmake lint-model                                 # import boundaries incl. SCP-02 for Umpire.Case.Producer\\ngo test -tags test_dep ./tools/umpire/...       # tutorial-drift and umpire-run unit tests\\nmake umpire-check-live-tests                    # tagged live Cases against the functional cluster\\nmake umpire-check-regression                    # the full gate\\n```\\n\\n## Acceptance Criteria\\n\\n- **R1:** `Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Success.Producer` no longer exists; the success Model file\\n  ends in a `case` block; `Umpire.Case.Producer` takes the Umpire-owned input record and passes\\n  `lint-model` under MOD-01 and SCP-02; the extraction step regenerates the async-Nexus fixture\\n  byte-identical, and the `case` step regenerates it with a diff limited to the Case ID, Program\\n  ID, Contract ID, run-scope literal, and Provenance, listed in the receipt; two Models in\\n  different files get distinct Definition IDs and sources, pinned by `#guard`; both live\\n  async-Nexus tests pass. Errors: `verify` Query, unmapped selected Action, evidence for an\\n  unselected Action, and no witness each reject with a located message pinned by `#guard_msgs`.\\n- **R2:** `Temporal.Case.Template.nexusOperation` (sync and async) and `.workflow` exist as Lean\\n  values with hooks `start` and `completion` and their evidence sources; the sync form's history\\n  read waits for the close event before the full read. Errors: an unknown history event kind in\\n  `evidence` rejects listing the admitted kinds, pinned by `#guard_msgs`; no other error surface.\\n- **R3:** the worker-outage Case file no longer exists; the outage Case is a Model file with two\\n  `fault` lines; a Producer unit test asserts the rewritten dependency edges for `before` and\\n  `after`; both live outage tests pass with the outage-order rule (same ID, terminal state\\n  `resumed`) and every clause rule satisfied; the artifact test pins the `rule_events` Deadline.\\n  Errors: unknown hook and duplicate fault line reject with located messages pinned by\\n  `#guard_msgs`; unpaired stop is unchecked at elaboration and answered by the outage-order rule\\n  at Run time.\\n- **R4:** the Go generator has no functional Case table and no fixed-count guard, and the Lean\\n  renderer has no functional dispatch table; `umpire-case --list` prints every checked-in\\n  functional fixture name sorted, and nothing else; the shared artifact test is table-driven over\\n  the testdata directory; `runCase` derives a default binding from the fixture name. Errors:\\n  duplicate fixture name rejects at the materializing elaborator, pinned by `#guard_msgs`;\\n  `--render` of an unknown ID exits non-zero naming the known IDs.\\n- **R5:** `model/AUTHORING.md` exists and the old tutorial does not; a Go test asserts each Lean\\n  block in it equals the marked region of the R6 Model file. Errors: a missing or duplicate\\n  marker fails that test naming the marker; no other error surface.\\n- **R6:** one Model file translates `TestNexusOperationSyncCompletion` using only the six\\n  commands and its `inductive` declarations; `COVERAGE.md` beside it maps every upstream assertion\\n  to a Property clause, a Contract rule, or a Known Gap, with the mutable-state assertion recorded\\n  as a white-box Known Gap; the commit that adds it touches exactly two authored files; its live\\n  test passes. Errors: no error surface beyond R1 to R4.\\n- **R7:** `go build ./tools/umpire/cmd/umpire-run` succeeds without linking `tests/testcore` or\\n  `service/`; the provisioning package takes no test environment or `testing.T`; a tagged test\\n  runs the binary against the functional cluster with the async-Nexus fixture and asserts exit 0.\\n  Errors: exit 1, 2, and 3 covered by unit tests through a fake Driver; unreachable address,\\n  missing fixture, typed fixture, existing resource with `--create`, timeout, and SIGINT each exit\\n  3 with one stderr line naming the cause and any leaked resource.\\n- **R8:** `make umpire-check-regression` passes; `make lint-code` reports no finding in a file\\n  this spec touched; `lint-model` stays at or below its inherited count; the roadmap and the\\n  documents the docs-gap scan named are updated. Errors: no error surface.\\n\\n## Early proof point\\n\\nTask .1 extracts the generic Producer and proves it by regenerating the async-Nexus fixture\\nbyte-identical through the extracted code. If that needs a Nexus-specific branch inside\\n`Umpire.Case.Producer`, stop: the template boundary is wrong or fn-80's derivation was never\\ngeneric, and R2 through R7 build on it.\\n\\n## Requirement coverage\\n\\n| Req | Description | Task(s) | Gap justification |\\n|-----|-------------|---------|-------------------|\\n| R1 | `case` block, generic Producer, per-file families, Nexus success Producer deleted | .1, .2, .3 | \\u2014 |\\n| R2 | realization templates, evidence sources, event kind resolution | .2, .6 | \\u2014 |\\n| R3 | fault lines with edge rewriting, worker-outage re-authored | .5 | \\u2014 |\\n| R4 | Case registry, generator table deleted, artifact and live-test helpers | .3, .4 | \\u2014 |\\n| R5 | authoring walkthrough with drift test | .8 | \\u2014 |\\n| R6 | translated upstream test with coverage record | .6 | \\u2014 |\\n| R7 | provisioning package and `umpire-run` | .7 | \\u2014 |\\n| R8 | full gate, documents, roadmap | .8 | \\u2014 |\\n\\n## Boundaries\\n\\n- **Exploration and fuzzing** stay in fn-33, which gains this spec as a dependency for the\\n  generic Producer. Go admission\\nneither validates nor reads them: the STATE step condition reads `State` plus `StateFields` only.\\nThe investigation settled which. No rule condition can read a prior or initial state (there is no\\nprior step field), but Lean's correlated monitor does read both fields: it decodes every state as a\\n`StateValue` (the atom plus its fields), starts from the initial `StateValue`, and takes a transition\\nonly when its prior `StateValue`, fields included, equals the current one. Go's monitor tracks the\\natom alone, validates none of the three field lists (`state_fields` included), and its output-row\\nequality and continuity ignore fields where Lean compares them. For Lean-produced Cases the fields\\nfollow from the atom, so the corpus agrees today; a Case whose fields disagree is where Go and Lean\\ndiverge. Every behavior the\\ncorpus, the conformance tests and the live identities pin stays the same.\\n\\n## Architecture & Data Models\\n<!-- scope: technical -->\\n\\nLane E comes first, then identity pins, dead code (A), validate-once (C), shared primitives (B),\\nthe internals (D1, D2), tests (F) and the protocol arms (G).
